```
+/// Dart's tree-sitter grammar only accepts declarations at the top level.
+/// Expression-level patterns (function calls, member access, assignments, etc.)
+/// fail to parse when given as bare source, since they are not valid top-level
+/// Dart constructs. This implementation wraps such patterns inside a function
+/// body and extracts the inner node so that expression/statement patterns work.
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
+pub struct Dart;

+const WRAPPER_PREFIX: &str = "void _() {\n";
+const WRAPPER_SUFFIX: &str = "\n}";

+impl Language for Dart {
+  fn kind_to_id(&self, kind: &str) -> u16 {
+    self
+      .get_ts_language()
+      .id_for_node_kind(kind, true)
+  }
+  fn field_to_id(&self, field: &str) -> Option<u16> {
+    self
+      .get_ts_language()
+      .field_id_for_name(field)
+      .map(|f| f.get())
+  }
+  fn build_pattern(&self, builder: &PatternBuilder) -> Result<Pattern, PatternError> {
+    let src = builder.src();
+    // Check if the pattern parses cleanly as a top-level construct.
+    // Dart's grammar can misinterpret expressions as declarations
+    // (e.g. `print($A)` becomes a function_signature, `AppIcons.$FIELD`
+    // becomes a type reference).  If the direct parse has any ERROR or
+    // MISSING nodes, try wrapping in a function body instead.
+    if self.should_try_wrapping(src) {
+      if let Some(pattern) = self.try_wrapped_pattern(src) {
+        return Ok(pattern);
+      }
+    }
+    builder.build(|s| StrDoc::try_new(s, *self))
+  }
 }
+/// Dart keywords that begin statements but are not valid function/type names.
+/// When tree-sitter misinterprets `if (...)` as a function declaration named
+/// "if", this list lets us detect and correct the misparse.
+const DART_STATEMENT_KEYWORDS: &[&str] = &[
+  "if", "else", "for", "while", "do", "switch", "try", "throw", "rethrow",
+  "return", "break", "continue", "yield", "await", "assert",
+];
+
+impl Dart {
+  /// Decides whether the direct (top-level) parse is likely wrong and
+  /// wrapping should be attempted.  Four independent signals:
+  ///  1. Root node itself is ERROR (total misparse)
+  ///  2. A direct child of source_file is ERROR
+  ///  3. Any MISSING node in the tree (grammar hallucinated a token like `;`)
+  ///  4. Source starts with a statement keyword misread as a function name
+  fn should_try_wrapping(&self, src: &str) -> bool {
+    let Ok(doc) = StrDoc::try_new(src, *self) else {
+      return true;
+    };
+    let root = Root::doc(doc);
+    let root_node = root.root();
+    if root_node.is_error() {
+      return true;
+    }
+    if root_node.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {
+      return true;
+    }
+    if Self::subtree_has_missing(&root_node) {
+      return true;
+    }
+    let first_word = src.split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric() && c != '_').next().unwrap_or("");
+    DART_STATEMENT_KEYWORDS.contains(&first_word)
+  }
+
+  fn subtree_has_missing<D: ast_grep_core::Doc>(node: &ast_grep_core::Node<'_, D>) -> bool {
+    if node.is_missing() {
+      return true;
+    }
+    node.children().any(|c| Self::subtree_has_missing(&c))
+  }

-#[test]
-fn test_dart_class_with_body() {
-  test_match("class $A { $$$BODY }", "class Foo { int x = 1; }");
+  fn try_wrapped_pattern(&self, src: &str) -> Option<Pattern> {
+    // Try without semicolon first (statement patterns like if/for/while),
+    // then with semicolon (expression patterns that need `;` to form a
+    // complete statement in Dart).
+    let candidates = [
+      format!("{WRAPPER_PREFIX}{src}{WRAPPER_SUFFIX}"),
+      format!("{WRAPPER_PREFIX}{src};{WRAPPER_SUFFIX}"),
+    ];
+    for wrapped in &candidates {
+      if let Some(pattern) = self.try_extract_inner(wrapped) {
+        return Some(pattern);
+      }
+    }
+    None
+  }
+
+  fn try_extract_inner(&self, wrapped: &str) -> Option<Pattern> {
+    let doc = StrDoc::try_new(wrapped, *self).ok()?;
+    let root = Root::doc(doc);
+    let root_node = root.root();
+    let block_kind = KindMatcher::new("block", *self);
+    let block = root_node.find(&block_kind)?;
+    // Reject if the block contains any ERROR nodes — that means the
+    // pattern didn't parse cleanly as a statement/expression.
+    if block.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {
+      return None;
+    }
+    let inner = block.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?;
+    // If the wrapper added a semicolon, the extracted node is an
+    // expression_statement.  Unwrap to the inner expression so that
+    // `print($A)` matches `call_expression` nodes directly.
+    let target = if inner.kind() == "expression_statement" {
+      inner.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?
+    } else {
+      inner
+    };
+    let pattern = Pattern::from(target);
+    if pattern.has_error() {
+      return None;
+    }
+    Some(pattern)
+  }
 }

+mod test {
+  use super::*;

+

+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_debug_parse_kinds() {
+    let patterns = [
+      "print($A)",
+      "AppIcons.$FIELD",
+      "OverlayButton.bubble($$$)",
+      "$A = $B",
+      "var $A = $B",
+      "if ($COND) { $$$BODY }",
+      "void $NAME($$$) { $$$BODY }",
+      "import $URI",
+      "class $A {}",
+      "$X.add($A)",
+      "return $A",
+    ];
+    for pat in patterns {
+      let doc = StrDoc::try_new(pat, Dart).unwrap();
+      let root = Root::doc(doc);
+      let root_node = root.root();
+      fn dump<D: ast_grep_core::Doc>(node: &ast_grep_core::Node<'_, D>, depth: usize) {
+        let indent = "  ".repeat(depth);
+        let err_flag = if node.is_error() { " [ERROR]" } else if node.is_missing() { " [MISSING]" } else { "" };
+        eprintln!("{}{}{}", indent, node.kind(), err_flag);
+        for c in node.children() {
+          dump(&c, depth + 1);
+        }
+      }
+      eprintln!("PATTERN: {:?} root_kind={} is_error={}", pat, root_node.kind(), root_node.is_error());
+      eprintln!();
+    }
+  }
+

+
+  #[test]

+
+  #[test]
```

```
103	    let root = Root::doc(doc);
104	    let root_node = root.root();
105	    let block_kind = KindMatcher::new("block", *self);
106	    let block = root_node.find(&block_kind)?;
107	    // Reject if the block contains any ERROR nodes — that means the
108	    // pattern didn't parse cleanly as a statement/expression.
109	    if block.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {
110	      return None;
111	    }
112	    let inner = block.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?;
113	    // If the wrapper added a semicolon, the extracted node is an
114	    // expression_statement.  Unwrap to the inner expression so that
115	    // `print($A)` matches `call_expression` nodes directly.
116	    let target = if inner.kind() == "expression_statement" {
117	      inner.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?
118	    } else {
119	      inner
120	    };
121	    let pattern = Pattern::from(target);
122	    if pattern.has_error() {
123	      return None;
124	    }
125	    Some(pattern)
126	  }
127	}

130	  fn get_ts_language(&self) -> TSLanguage {
131	    crate::parsers::language_dart().into()
132	  }
```

```
/usr/bin/flutter/bin/dart: Bourne-Again shell script, ASCII text executable
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Copyright 2014 The Flutter Authors. All rights reserved.
# Use of this source code is governed by a BSD-style license that can be
# found in the LICENSE file.

# ---------------------------------- NOTE ---------------------------------- #
#
# Please keep the logic in this file consistent with the logic in the
# `dart.bat` script in the same directory to ensure that Flutter & Dart continue
# to work across all platforms!
#
# -------------------------------------------------------------------------- #

set -e

# Needed because if it is set, cd may print the path it changed to.
unset CDPATH

# On Mac OS, readlink -f doesn't work, so follow_links traverses the path one
# link at a time, and then cds into the link destination and find out where it
# ends up.
#
# The returned filesystem path must be a format usable by Dart's URI parser,
# since the Dart command line tool treats its argument as a file URI, not a
# filename. For instance, multiple consecutive slashes should be reduced to a
# single slash, since double-slashes indicate a URI "authority", and these are
# supposed to be filenames. There is an edge case where this will return
# multiple slashes: when the input resolves to the root directory. However, if
# that were the case, we wouldn't be running this shell, so we don't do anything
# about it.
#
# The function is enclosed in a subshell to avoid changing the working directory
# of the caller.
function follow_links() (
  cd -P "$(dirname -- "$1")"
  file="$PWD/$(basename -- "$1")"
  while [[ -h "$file" ]]; do
    cd -P "$(dirname -- "$file")"
    file="$(readlink -- "$file")"
    cd -P "$(dirname -- "$file")"
    file="$PWD/$(basename -- "$file")"
  done
  echo "$file"
)

PROG_NAME="$(follow_links "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")"
BIN_DIR="$(cd "${PROG_NAME%/*}" ; pwd -P)"
SHARED_NAME="$BIN_DIR/internal/shared.sh"
OS="$(uname -s)"

# If we're on Windows, invoke the batch script instead to get proper locking.
if [[ $OS =~ MINGW.* || $OS =~ CYGWIN.* || $OS =~ MSYS.* ]]; then
  exec "${BIN_DIR}/dart.bat" "$@"
fi

# To define `shared::execute()` function
source "$SHARED_NAME"

shared::execute "$@"
```

7	## What changed since the prior handoff
8
9	The prior session left the code with a `direct_parse_has_errors` function that did a **full recursive subtree scan** for ERROR/MISSING nodes. That caused 3 test failures because metavariable placeholders like `$$$BODY` create ERROR nodes deep inside otherwise-valid declaration patterns.
10
11	This session replaced that with a multi-signal heuristic called `should_try_wrapping`. The function is in `/home/me/Desktop/workspace/ast-grep/crates/language/src/dart.rs`.
12
13	### The four signals in `should_try_wrapping`
14
15	1. **Root node is ERROR** — the parse totally failed (e.g. `AppIcons.$FIELD` produces ERROR as the root, not `source_file`)
16	2. **Direct child of source_file is ERROR** — top-level misparse (e.g. `print($A)` wraps in ERROR at the source_file level)
17	3. **Any MISSING node anywhere in the tree** — the grammar hallucinated a token (most commonly a missing semicolon `;`). This catches patterns like `$A = $B`, `var $A = $B`, `OverlayButton.bubble($$$)` which tree-sitter incorrectly interprets as top-level variable/function declarations with a missing semicolon
18	4. **Source starts with a Dart statement keyword** — catches `if`, `for`, `while`, `return`, etc. being misinterpreted as function names. The keyword list is in `DART_STATEMENT_KEYWORDS` constant at the top of the `impl Dart` block
19
20	### Candidate ordering was also swapped
21
22	The `try_wrapped_pattern` method tries two wrapping candidates:
23	- **Without semicolon first**: `void _() {\n<pattern>\n}` — for statement patterns (if/for/while)
24	- **With semicolon second**: `void _() {\n<pattern>;\n}` — for expression patterns (function calls, assignments)
25
26	The prior code had the with-semicolon candidate first. That caused the `if ($COND) { $$$BODY }` test to fail because adding `;` after `}` made tree-sitter parse `{ $$$BODY }` as a set/map literal instead of a block (Dart grammar ambiguity). The without-semicolon candidate parses it correctly as an `if_statement`.
27
28	Expression patterns like `print($A)` still work: the without-semicolon attempt fails (Dart requires `;` after expressions — the block gets ERROR children), so it falls through to the with-semicolon candidate which succeeds.
29
30	## Current test results: 13 pass, 2 fail
31

33
34	**Passing (13)**: class, class_with_body, replace, function_call, method_call, named_constructor, assignment, variable_declaration, member_access, return_statement, top_level_function, expression_replace, debug_parse_kinds
35
36	**Failing (2)**:
37
38	### `test_dart_import` — `import $URI`
39
40	Pattern `import $URI` at top level parses as `top_level_variable_declaration(type: import, name: $URI)` with MISSING `;`. The MISSING `;` triggers `should_try_wrapping`, but the wrapped version also fails because `import` is a keyword that cannot appear inside a function body as a statement.
41
42	This test is probably a bad test case (as noted in the prior handoff). `import` statements are top-level-only constructs in Dart. The pattern `import $URI` can never match inside a function body, and at the top level the grammar expects `import 'string_literal'` not `import $URI` (because `$URI` is treated as an identifier, not a string). The correct way to match imports in ast-grep would be using `--selector` with the `import_specification` node kind, or a pattern like `import '$URI'` with the quotes included.
43
44	**Recommendation**: Either fix the test to use `import '$URI'` (but `$` inside Dart strings is interpolation syntax, so this might also fail), or remove/skip this test and document that imports need `--selector` matching.
45
46	### `test_dart_if_statement` — `if ($COND) { $$$BODY }`
47
48	The `should_try_wrapping` function correctly identifies this needs wrapping (signal 4: starts with `if`). The wrapping logic tries the without-semicolon candidate first. **I did not get to run the tests after swapping the candidate order for this specific test** — the session was interrupted right after making that change. The swap should fix this test, but it needs verification.
49
50	If it still fails: the issue would be in `try_extract_inner`. The `{ $$$BODY }` inside the wrapped `if_statement` might produce ERROR nodes that cause the block-level check (`block.children().any(|c| c.is_error())`) to reject the extraction. Check whether `$$$BODY` (which is a valid Dart identifier due to `$` being legal) is parsed cleanly inside the if-statement's consequence block, or whether `$$$` triggers some other error.

```
118	pub fn match_node_non_recursive<'tree, D: Doc>(
119	  goal: &Pattern,
120	  candidate: Node<'tree, D>,
121	  env: &mut Cow<MetaVarEnv<'tree, D>>,
122	) -> Option<Node<'tree, D>> {
123	  match match_node_impl(&goal.node, &candidate, env, &goal.strictness) {
124	    MatchOneNode::MatchedBoth => Some(candidate),
125	    _ => None,
126	  }
127	}
128
129	pub fn does_node_match_exactly<D: Doc>(goal: &Node<D>, candidate: &Node<D>) -> bool {
130	  // return true if goal and candidate are the same node
131	  if goal.node_id() == candidate.node_id() {
132	    return true;
133	  }
134	  // gh issue #1087, we make pattern matching a little bit more permissive
135	  // compare node text if at least one node is leaf
136	  if goal.is_named_leaf() || candidate.is_named_leaf() {
137	    return goal.text() == candidate.text();
138	  }
139	  if goal.kind_id() != candidate.kind_id() {
140	    return false;
141	  }
142	  let goal_children = goal.children();
143	  let cand_children = candidate.children();
144	  if goal_children.len() != cand_children.len() {
145	    return false;
146	  }
147	  goal_children
148	    .zip(cand_children)
149	    .all(|(g, c)| does_node_match_exactly(&g, &c))
150	}
```

```
8	pub(super) fn match_node_impl<'tree, D: Doc>(
9	  goal: &PatternNode,

18	      text,
19	      kind_id,
20	      is_named,
21	    } => match strictness.match_terminal(*is_named, text, *kind_id, candidate) {
22	      MatchOneNode::MatchedBoth => {
23	        if agg.match_terminal(candidate).is_some() {
24	          MatchOneNode::MatchedBoth
25	        } else {
26	          MatchOneNode::NoMatch
27	        }

30	    },
31	    P::MetaVar { meta_var, .. } => {

33	        return MatchOneNode::SkipCandidate;
34	      }

41	      kind_id, children, ..
42	    } => {
43	      let kind_matched = strictness.should_skip_kind()
44	        || kind_utils::are_kinds_matching(*kind_id, candidate.kind_id());

46	        return MatchOneNode::NoMatch;
47	      }
48	      let cand_children = candidate.children();
49	      match match_nodes_impl_recursive(children, cand_children, agg, strictness) {
50	        Some(()) => MatchOneNode::MatchedBoth,
51	        None => MatchOneNode::NoMatch,
52	      }
53	    }
54	  }
55	}
```

```
57	fn match_nodes_impl_recursive<'tree, D: Doc>(
58	  goals: &[PatternNode],
59	  candidates: impl Iterator<Item = Node<'tree, D>>,
60	  agg: &mut impl Aggregator<'tree, D>,
61	  strictness: &MatchStrictness,
62	) -> Option<()> {
63	  let mut goal_children = goals.iter().peekable();
64	  let mut cand_children = candidates.peekable();
65	  cand_children.peek()?;
66	  loop {
67	    match may_match_ellipsis_impl(&mut goal_children, &mut cand_children, agg, strictness)? {
68	      ControlFlow::Return => return Some(()),
69	      ControlFlow::Continue => continue,
70	      ControlFlow::Fallthrough => (),
71	    }
72	    match match_single_node_while_skip_trivial(
73	      &mut goal_children,
74	      &mut cand_children,
75	      agg,
76	      strictness,
77	    )? {
78	      ControlFlow::Return => return Some(()),
79	      ControlFlow::Continue => continue,
80	      ControlFlow::Fallthrough => (),
81	    }
82	    let consumed_goal = goal_children.next();
83	    // if goal runs out, do not proceed cand nodes
84	    if consumed_goal.is_some() {
85	      cand_children.next();
86	    }
87	    if goal_children.peek().is_none() {
88	      // all goal found
89	      let has_trailing = cand_children.all(|n| strictness.should_skip_trailing(&n));
90	      return has_trailing.then_some(());
91	    }
92	    cand_children.peek()?;
93	  }
94	}

96	enum ControlFlow {
97	  Continue,
98	  Fallthrough,
99	  Return,
100	}
101
102	/// returns None means no match
103	fn may_match_ellipsis_impl<'p, 't: 'p, D: Doc>(
104	  goal_children: &mut Peekable<impl Iterator<Item = &'p PatternNode>>,
105	  cand_children: &mut Peekable<impl Iterator<Item = Node<'t, D>>>,
106	  agg: &mut impl Aggregator<'t, D>,
107	  strictness: &MatchStrictness,
108	) -> Option<ControlFlow> {
109	  let Some(curr_node) = goal_children.peek() else {
110	    // in rare case, an internal node's children is empty
111	    // see https://github.com/ast-grep/ast-grep/issues/1688
112	    return Some(ControlFlow::Return);
113	  };
114	  let Ok(optional_name) = try_get_ellipsis_mode(curr_node) else {
115	    return Some(ControlFlow::Fallthrough);
116	  };
117	  let mut matched = vec![];
118	  goal_children.next();
119	  // goal has all matched
120	  if goal_children.peek().is_none() {
121	    match_ellipsis(agg, &optional_name, matched, cand_children, 0)?;
122	    return Some(ControlFlow::Return);
123	  }
124	  // skip trivial nodes in goal after ellipsis
125	  let mut skipped_anonymous = 0;
126	  while goal_children.peek().unwrap().is_trivial() {
127	    goal_children.next();
128	    skipped_anonymous += 1;
129	    if goal_children.peek().is_none() {
130	      match_ellipsis(
131	        agg,
132	        &optional_name,
133	        matched,
134	        cand_children,
135	        skipped_anonymous,
136	      )?;
```

**Found it!** In the wrapped parse, the if_statement's consequence is parsed as `expression_statement > set_or_map_literal` instead of `block`. Dart's grammar is ambiguous about `{ ... }` — it could be a block or a set/map literal. When the pattern content `$$$BODY` looks like an identifier, the grammar chooses set/map literal.

The candidate's if_statement has `block` (kind_id=313) as the consequence, but the pattern has `expression_statement > set_or_map_literal` (kind_id=314 > 224). Kind mismatch = no match.

```
55	  fn contextual<D: Doc>(&self, root: &Root<D>, selector: &str) -> Result<Pattern, PatternError> {
56	    let goal = root.root();
57	    let kind_matcher = KindMatcher::try_new(selector, root.lang().clone())?;
58	    let Some(node) = goal.find(&kind_matcher) else {
59	      return Err(PatternError::NoSelectorInContext {
60	        context: self.src.to_string(),
61	        selector: selector.into(),
62	      });
63	    };
64	    Ok(Pattern {
65	      root_kind: Some(node.kind_id()),
66	      node: convert_node_to_pattern(node.get_node().clone()),
67	      strictness: MatchStrictness::Smart,
68	    })
69	  }
70	}
```

```json
  "old_string": "  fn try_extract_inner(&self, wrapped: &str) -> Option<Pattern> {\n    let doc = StrDoc::try_new(wrapped, *self).ok()?;\n    let root = Root::doc(doc);\n    let root_node = root.root();\n    let block_kind = KindMatcher::new(\"block\", *self);\n    let block = root_node.find(&block_kind)?;\n    // Reject if the block contains any ERROR nodes — that means the\n    // pattern didn't parse cleanly as a statement/expression.\n    eprintln!(\"DEBUG try_extract_inner wrapped={:?}\", wrapped);\n    for c in block.children() {\n      eprintln!(\"  block child: kind={} is_error={} is_named={} text={:?}\", c.kind(), c.is_error(), c.is_named(), c.text());\n    }\n    if block.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {\n      eprintln!(\"  => REJECTED (block has ERROR child)\");\n      return None;\n    }\n    let inner = block.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?;\n    eprintln!(\"  inner kind={} text={:?}\", inner.kind(), inner.text());\n    // If the wrapper added a semicolon, the extracted node is an\n    // expression_statement.  Unwrap to the inner expression so that\n    // `print($A)` matches `call_expression` nodes directly.\n    let target = if inner.kind() == \"expression_statement\" {\n      inner.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?\n    } else {\n      inner\n    };\n    eprintln!(\"  target kind={} kind_id={} text={:?}\", target.kind(), target.kind_id(), target.text());\n    let pattern = Pattern::from(target);\n    eprintln!(\"  pattern has_error={} pattern={:?}\", pattern.has_error(), pattern);\n    eprintln!(\"  pattern potential_kinds={:?}\", ast_grep_core::matcher::Matcher::potential_kinds(&pattern));\n    if pattern.has_error() {\n      eprintln!(\"  => REJECTED (pattern has error)\");\n      return None;\n    }\n    Some(pattern)\n  }",
  "new_string": "  fn try_extract_inner(&self, wrapped: &str) -> Option<Pattern> {\n    let doc = StrDoc::try_new(wrapped, *self).ok()?;\n    let root = Root::doc(doc);\n    let root_node = root.root();\n    let block_kind = KindMatcher::new(\"block\", *self);\n    let block = root_node.find(&block_kind)?;\n    if block.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {\n      return None;\n    }\n    let inner = block.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?;\n    // Unwrap expression_statement to get the inner expression so that\n    // `print($A)` matches `call_expression` nodes directly.\n    let target = if inner.kind() == \"expression_statement\" {\n      inner.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?\n    } else {\n      inner\n    };\n    let mut pattern = Pattern::from(target);\n    if pattern.has_error() {\n      return None;\n    }\n    // Dart's grammar sometimes parses `{ $$$BODY }` as set_or_map_literal\n    // instead of block (ambiguity when content looks like an expression).\n    // Fix up the pattern tree so it matches real block nodes.\n    let set_or_map_id = self.kind_to_id(\"set_or_map_literal\");\n    let block_id = self.kind_to_id(\"block\");\n    let expr_stmt_id = self.kind_to_id(\"expression_statement\");\n    Self::fix_set_literal_to_block(&mut pattern.node, set_or_map_id, block_id, expr_stmt_id);\n    Some(pattern)\n  }\n\n  /// Recursively replace set_or_map_literal nodes with block nodes in the\n  /// pattern tree. This fixes the Dart grammar ambiguity where `{ $$$BODY }`\n  /// is parsed as a set literal instead of a block in statement contexts.\n  fn fix_set_literal_to_block(\n    node: &mut PatternNode,\n    set_or_map_id: u16,\n    block_id: u16,\n    expr_stmt_id: u16,\n  ) {\n    if let PatternNode::Internal { kind_id, children } = node {\n      // An expression_statement wrapping a set_or_map_literal should\n      // become just a block (remove the expression_statement wrapper).\n      if *kind_id == expr_stmt_id {\n        if let Some(first_named) = children.iter().position(|c| {\n          matches!(c, PatternNode::Internal { kind_id: k, .. } if *k == set_or_map_id)\n        }) {\n          if let PatternNode::Internal { kind_id: inner_kind, children: inner_children } =\n            &mut children[first_named]\n          {\n            *inner_kind = block_id;\n            // Promote the block to replace the expression_statement\n            let block_children = std::mem::take(inner_children);\n            *kind_id = block_id;\n            *children = block_children;\n            return;\n          }\n        }\n      }\n      if *kind_id == set_or_map_id {\n        *kind_id = block_id;\n      }\n      for child in children.iter_mut() {\n        Self::fix_set_literal_to_block(child, set_or_map_id, block_id, expr_stmt_id);\n      }\n    }\n  }"
}
```

*2026-06-02 17:42:22 UTC*

Rust LSP (rust-analyzer) is working — hover info returns type info and docs for the `Dart` struct.

Here's the summary:

**Rust LSP (rust-analyzer)**: Installed and working. It was missing from the toolchain; I ran `rustup component add rust-analyzer` to install it.

**Dart LSP**: There's no Claude Code LSP plugin for Dart (only an MCP server for the Dart SDK). The Dart SDK binary at `/usr/bin/flutter/bin/dart` has a shell compatibility issue in this environment.

**Dart ast-grep support (the code changes)**: Fixed both test failures:
1. **`test_dart_if_statement`** — Dart's grammar parsed `{ $$$BODY }` as a `set_or_map_literal` instead of `block` inside if-statement consequences. Added `fix_set_literal_to_block` to post-process the pattern tree, replacing set_or_map_literal kind_ids with block kind_ids.
2. **`test_dart_import`** — Changed pattern from `import $URI` to `import '$URI'` since Dart's grammar expects string literals in import statements, not bare identifiers.

All 80 language tests and 645+ total tests pass with zero failures.

```
1	use ast_grep_core::Language;
2	use ast_grep_core::matcher::{KindMatcher, Pattern, PatternBuilder, PatternError, PatternNode};

4	use ast_grep_core::tree_sitter::{LanguageExt, StrDoc, TSLanguage};

6	/// Dart's tree-sitter grammar only accepts declarations at the top level.
7	/// Expression-level patterns (function calls, member access, assignments, etc.)
8	/// fail to parse when given as bare source, since they are not valid top-level
9	/// Dart constructs. This implementation wraps such patterns inside a function
10	/// body and extracts the inner node so that expression/statement patterns work.
11	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
12	pub struct Dart;
13
14	const WRAPPER_PREFIX: &str = "void _() {\n";
15	const WRAPPER_SUFFIX: &str = "\n}";
16
17	impl Language for Dart {
18	  fn kind_to_id(&self, kind: &str) -> u16 {
19	    self
20	      .get_ts_language()
21	      .id_for_node_kind(kind, true)
22	  }
23	  fn field_to_id(&self, field: &str) -> Option<u16> {
24	    self
25	      .get_ts_language()
26	      .field_id_for_name(field)
27	      .map(|f| f.get())
28	  }
29	  fn build_pattern(&self, builder: &PatternBuilder) -> Result<Pattern, PatternError> {
30	    let src = builder.src();
31	    // Check if the pattern parses cleanly as a top-level construct.
32	    // Dart's grammar can misinterpret expressions as declarations
33	    // (e.g. `print($A)` becomes a function_signature, `AppIcons.$FIELD`
34	    // becomes a type reference).  If the direct parse has any ERROR or
35	    // MISSING nodes, try wrapping in a function body instead.
36	    if self.should_try_wrapping(src) {
37	      if let Some(pattern) = self.try_wrapped_pattern(src) {
38	        return Ok(pattern);
39	      }
40	    }
41	    builder.build(|s| StrDoc::try_new(s, *self))
42	  }
43	}
44	/// Dart keywords that begin statements but are not valid function/type names.
45	/// When tree-sitter misinterprets `if (...)` as a function declaration named
46	/// "if", this list lets us detect and correct the misparse.
47	const DART_STATEMENT_KEYWORDS: &[&str] = &[
48	  "if", "else", "for", "while", "do", "switch", "try", "throw", "rethrow",
49	  "return", "break", "continue", "yield", "await", "assert",
50	];
51
52	impl Dart {
53	  /// Decides whether the direct (top-level) parse is likely wrong and
54	  /// wrapping should be attempted.  Four independent signals:
55	  ///  1. Root node itself is ERROR (total misparse)
56	  ///  2. A direct child of source_file is ERROR
57	  ///  3. Any MISSING node in the tree (grammar hallucinated a token like `;`)
58	  ///  4. Source starts with a statement keyword misread as a function name
59	  fn should_try_wrapping(&self, src: &str) -> bool {
60	    let Ok(doc) = StrDoc::try_new(src, *self) else {
61	      return true;
62	    };
63	    let root = Root::doc(doc);
64	    let root_node = root.root();
65	    if root_node.is_error() {
66	      return true;
67	    }
68	    if root_node.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {
69	      return true;
70	    }
71	    if Self::subtree_has_missing(&root_node) {
72	      return true;
73	    }
74	    let first_word = src.split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric() && c != '_').next().unwrap_or("");
75	    DART_STATEMENT_KEYWORDS.contains(&first_word)
76	  }
77
78	  fn subtree_has_missing<D: ast_grep_core::Doc>(node: &ast_grep_core::Node<'_, D>) -> bool {
79	    if node.is_missing() {
80	      return true;
81	    }
82	    node.children().any(|c| Self::subtree_has_missing(&c))
83	  }
84
85	  fn try_wrapped_pattern(&self, src: &str) -> Option<Pattern> {
86	    // Try without semicolon first (statement patterns like if/for/while),
87	    // then with semicolon (expression patterns that need `;` to form a
88	    // complete statement in Dart).
89	    let candidates = [
90	      format!("{WRAPPER_PREFIX}{src}{WRAPPER_SUFFIX}"),
91	      format!("{WRAPPER_PREFIX}{src};{WRAPPER_SUFFIX}"),
92	    ];
93	    for wrapped in &candidates {
94	      if let Some(pattern) = self.try_extract_inner(wrapped) {
95	        return Some(pattern);
96	      }
97	    }
98	    None
99	  }
100
101	  fn try_extract_inner(&self, wrapped: &str) -> Option<Pattern> {
102	    let doc = StrDoc::try_new(wrapped, *self).ok()?;
103	    let root = Root::doc(doc);
104	    let root_node = root.root();
105	    let block_kind = KindMatcher::new("block", *self);
106	    let block = root_node.find(&block_kind)?;
107	    if block.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {
108	      return None;
109	    }
110	    let inner = block.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?;
111	    // Unwrap expression_statement to get the inner expression so that
112	    // `print($A)` matches `call_expression` nodes directly.
113	    let target = if inner.kind() == "expression_statement" {
114	      inner.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?
115	    } else {
116	      inner
117	    };
118	    let mut pattern = Pattern::from(target);
119	    if pattern.has_error() {
120	      return None;
121	    }
122	    // Dart's grammar sometimes parses `{ $$$BODY }` as set_or_map_literal
123	    // instead of block (ambiguity when content looks like an expression).
124	    // Fix up the pattern tree so it matches real block nodes.
125	    let set_or_map_id = self.kind_to_id("set_or_map_literal");
126	    let block_id = self.kind_to_id("block");
127	    let expr_stmt_id = self.kind_to_id("expression_statement");
128	    Self::fix_set_literal_to_block(&mut pattern.node, set_or_map_id, block_id, expr_stmt_id);
129	    Some(pattern)
130	  }
131
132	  /// Recursively replace set_or_map_literal nodes with block nodes in the
133	  /// pattern tree. This fixes the Dart grammar ambiguity where `{ $$$BODY }`
134	  /// is parsed as a set literal instead of a block in statement contexts.
135	  fn fix_set_literal_to_block(
136	    node: &mut PatternNode,
137	    set_or_map_id: u16,
138	    block_id: u16,
139	    expr_stmt_id: u16,
140	  ) {
141	    if let PatternNode::Internal { kind_id, children } = node {
142	      // An expression_statement wrapping a set_or_map_literal should
143	      // become just a block (remove the expression_statement wrapper).
144	      if *kind_id == expr_stmt_id {
145	        if let Some(first_named) = children.iter().position(|c| {
146	          matches!(c, PatternNode::Internal { kind_id: k, .. } if *k == set_or_map_id)
147	        }) {
148	          if let PatternNode::Internal { kind_id: inner_kind, children: inner_children } =
149	            &mut children[first_named]
150	          {
151	            *inner_kind = block_id;
152	            // Promote the block to replace the expression_statement
153	            let block_children = std::mem::take(inner_children);
154	            *kind_id = block_id;
155	            *children = block_children;
156	            return;
157	          }
158	        }
159	      }
160	      if *kind_id == set_or_map_id {
161	        *kind_id = block_id;
162	      }
163	      for child in children.iter_mut() {
164	        Self::fix_set_literal_to_block(child, set_or_map_id, block_id, expr_stmt_id);
165	      }
166	    }
167	  }
168	}
169

173	  }
174	}
```

1	---
2	name: oughta
3	description: GUI automation via the `oughta` CLI — mouse clicks, keyboard input, window lookup, and screenshots. Use for interacting with any desktop application.
4	argument-hint: <action> [args] — e.g. "clickOn 500 300" or "screenshot /tmp/out.png $WID"
5	allowed-tools: Bash(oughta *), Bash(DISPLAY=:0 oughta *), Bash(identify *), Bash(convert *)
6	---
7
8	# Oughta — GUI Automation
9
10	`oughta` is a lightweight X11 GUI automation tool.
11
12	## Key syntax gotchas
13
14	- **`clickOn <x> <y>`** — moves to position then clicks. Use this for clicking at coordinates.
15	- **`click [<btn>] [<x>] [<y>]`** — btn is the FIRST arg (defaults to left). Do NOT pass bare coordinates to `click` — it will interpret x as the button number and fail with BadValue.
16
17	## Actions
18
19	```bash
20	# Find window ID by WM_CLASS (case-insensitive)
21	oughta wid <class>
22
23	# Click at absolute screen coordinates
24	oughta clickOn <x> <y>
25
26	# Move mouse without clicking
27	oughta moveTo <x> <y>
28
29	# Click a specific mouse button at a position
30	oughta click left <x> <y>
31	oughta click right <x> <y>
32
33	# Type text into the focused window
34	oughta mash "some text"
35
36	# Press a special key
37	oughta press Escape
38	oughta press Return
39	oughta ESC
40	oughta ENTER
41
42	# Screenshot a window (optional crop geometry: WxH+X+Y)
43	oughta screenshot /tmp/out.png "$WID"
44	oughta screenshot /tmp/out.png "$WID" "700x30+0+88"
45
46	# Get current cursor position
47	oughta pos
48	```
49
50	## Typical workflow
51
52	```bash
53	WID=$(DISPLAY=:0 oughta wid <appclass>)
54	DISPLAY=:0 oughta clickOn <x> <y>
55	sleep 1
56	DISPLAY=:0 oughta screenshot /tmp/shot.png "$WID"
57	# Read /tmp/shot.png to inspect
58	```
59
60	Set `DISPLAY=:0` (or the appropriate display) before each command when running from a non-GUI terminal.
61
~~~~

```
Run one time search or rewrite in command line. (default command)

Usage: ast-grep run [OPTIONS] <--pattern <PATTERN>|--selector <KIND>|--strictness <STRICTNESS>|--kind <KIND>> [PATHS]...

Arguments:
  [PATHS]...
          The paths to search. You can provide multiple paths separated by spaces

          [default: .]

Options:
  -p, --pattern <PATTERN>
          AST pattern to match

      --selector <KIND>
          AST kind to extract sub-part of pattern to match.

          selector defines the sub-syntax node kind that is the actual matcher of the pattern. See https://ast-grep.github.io/guide/rule-config/atomic-rule.html#pattern-object.

      --strictness <STRICTNESS>
          The strictness of the pattern.

          See https://ast-grep.github.io/guide/rule-config/atomic-rule.html#strictness

          Possible values:
          - cst:       Match all nodes exactly
          - smart:     Match all nodes except source trivial nodes
          - ast:       Match only ast nodes
          - relaxed:   Match ast nodes except comments
          - signature: Match ast nodes except comments, without text
          - template:  Similar to smart but match text only, node kinds are ignored

  -k, --kind <KIND>
          AST kind to match.

          It accepts ESQuery style selector. See https://ast-grep.github.io/guide/rule-config/atomic-rule.html#esquery-style-kind

  -r, --rewrite <FIX>
          String to replace the matched AST node

  -l, --lang <LANG>
          The language of the pattern. For full language list, visit https://ast-grep.github.io/reference/languages.html

  -c, --config <CONFIG_FILE>
          Path to ast-grep root config, default is sgconfig.yml

      --debug-query[=<format>]
          Print query pattern's tree-sitter AST. Requires lang be set explicitly

          Possible values:
          - pattern: Print the query parsed in Pattern format
          - ast:     Print the query in tree-sitter AST format, only named nodes are shown
          - cst:     Print the query in tree-sitter CST format, both named and unnamed nodes are shown
          - sexp:    Print the query in S-expression format

      --follow
          Follow symbolic links.

          This flag instructs ast-grep to follow symbolic links while traversing directories. This behavior is disabled by default. Note that ast-grep will check for symbolic link loops and report errors if it finds one. ast-grep will also report errors for broken links.

      --no-ignore <FILE_TYPE>
          Do not respect hidden file system or ignore files (.gitignore, .ignore, etc.).

          You can suppress multiple ignore files by passing `no-ignore` multiple times.

          Possible values:
          - hidden:  Search hidden files and directories. By default, hidden files and directories are skipped
          - dot:     Don't respect .ignore files. This does *not* affect whether ast-grep will ignore files and directories whose names begin with a dot. For that, use --no-ignore hidden
          - exclude: Don't respect ignore files that are manually configured for the repository such as git's '.git/info/exclude'
          - global:  Don't respect ignore files that come from "global" sources such as git's `core.excludesFile` configuration option (which defaults to `$HOME/.config/git/ignore`)
          - parent:  Don't respect ignore files (.gitignore, .ignore, etc.) in parent directories
          - vcs:     Don't respect version control ignore files (.gitignore, etc.). This implies --no-ignore parent for VCS files. Note that .ignore files will continue to be respected

      --stdin
          Enable search code from StdIn.

          Use this if you need to take code stream from standard input.

      --globs <GLOBS>
          Include or exclude file paths.

          Include or exclude files and directories for searching that match the given glob. This always overrides any other ignore logic. Multiple glob flags may be used. Globbing rules match .gitignore globs. Precede a glob with a ! to exclude it. If multiple globs match a file or directory, the glob given later in the command line takes precedence.

  -j, --threads <NUM>
          Set the approximate number of threads to use.

          This flag sets the approximate number of threads to use. A value of 0 (which is the default) causes ast-grep to choose the thread count using heuristics.

          [default: 0]

  -i, --interactive
          Start interactive edit session.

          You can confirm the code change and apply it to files selectively, or you can open text editor to tweak the matched code. Note that code rewrite only happens inside a session.

  -U, --update-all
          Apply all rewrite without confirmation if true

      --files-with-matches
          Print only the paths with at least one match and suppress match contents.

          It conflicts with both the --interactive, --json and --update-all flags.

      --json[=<STYLE>]
          Output matches in structured JSON.

          If this flag is set, ast-grep will output matches in JSON format. You can pass optional value to this flag by using `--json=<STYLE>` syntax to further control how JSON object is formatted and printed. ast-grep will `pretty`-print JSON if no value is passed. Note, the json flag must use `=` to specify its value. It conflicts with interactive.

          Possible values:
          - pretty:  Prints the matches as a pretty-printed JSON array, with indentation and line breaks. This is useful for human readability, but not for parsing by other programs. This is the default value for the `--json` option
          - stream:  Prints each match as a separate JSON object, followed by a newline character. This is useful for streaming the output to other programs that can read one object per line
          - compact: Prints the matches as a single-line JSON array, without any whitespace. This is useful for saving space and minimizing the output size

      --color <WHEN>
          Controls output color.

          This flag controls when to use colors. The default setting is 'auto', which means ast-grep will try to guess when to use colors. If ast-grep is printing to a terminal, then it will use colors, but if it is redirected to a file or a pipe, then it will suppress color output. ast-grep will also suppress color output in some other circumstances. For example, no color will be used if the TERM environment variable is not set or set to 'dumb'.

          Possible values:
          - auto:   Try to use colors, but don't force the issue. If the output is piped to another program, or the console isn't available on Windows, or if TERM=dumb, or if `NO_COLOR` is defined, for example, then don't use colors
          - always: Try very hard to emit colors. This includes emitting ANSI colors on Windows if the console API is unavailable (not implemented yet)
          - ansi:   Ansi is like Always, except it never tries to use anything other than emitting ANSI color codes
          - never:  Never emit colors

          [default: auto]

      --inspect <GRANULARITY>
          Inspect information for file/rule discovery and scanning.

          This flag helps user to observe ast-grep's internal filtering of files and rules. Inspection will output how many and why files and rules are scanned and skipped. Inspection outputs to stderr and does not affect the result of the search.

          Possible values:
          - nothing: Do not show any tracing information
          - summary: Show summary about how many files are scanned and skipped
          - entity:  Show per-file/per-rule tracing information

          [default: nothing]

  -A, --after <NUM>
          Show NUM lines after each match.

          It conflicts with both the -C/--context flag.

          [default: 0]

  -B, --before <NUM>
          Show NUM lines before each match.

          It conflicts with both the -C/--context flag.

          [default: 0]

  -C, --context <NUM>
          Show NUM lines around each match.

          This is equivalent to providing both the -B/--before and -A/--after flags with the same value. It conflicts with both the -B/--before and -A/--after flags.

          [default: 0]

      --heading <WHEN>
          Controls whether to print the file name as heading.

          If heading is used, the file name will be printed as heading before all matches of that file. If heading is not used, ast-grep will print the file path before each match as prefix. The default value `auto` is to use heading when printing to a terminal and to disable heading when piping to another program or redirected to files.

          Possible values:
          - auto:   Print heading for terminal tty but not for piped output
          - always: Always print heading regardless of output type
          - never:  Never print heading regardless of output type

          [default: auto]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

~~~~json
{
  "file_path": "/home/me/.claude/skills/ast-grep-dart/SKILL.md",
  "content": "---\nname: ast-grep-dart\ndescription: Use ast-grep for structural code search and rewrite in Dart/Flutter projects. Invoke when asked to find, match, replace, or refactor Dart code patterns across a codebase — especially when grep/regex would be too fragile.\nargument-hint: <what to find or transform> — e.g. \"find all setState calls\" or \"replace print() with logger.d()\"\nallowed-tools: Bash(ast-grep *)\n---\n\n# ast-grep for Dart / Flutter\n\n`ast-grep` (alias `sg`) searches and rewrites code using AST patterns — not regex.\nIt understands Dart syntax, so `print($A)` matches any single-argument `print()` call\nregardless of formatting, comments, or whitespace.\n\n## Core concepts\n\n- **`$X`** — captures a single named AST node (identifier, expression, type, etc.)\n- **`$$$`** — captures zero or more nodes (arguments, statements, fields)\n- **`$$_`** — anonymous multi-capture (don't bind a name, just match any run of nodes)\n- Patterns are structural: `$A.add($B)` matches `list.add(1)` but not `list.remove(1)`\n\n## Dart-specific quirks\n\nDart's grammar only allows declarations at the top level. ast-grep's Dart support\nautomatically wraps expression/statement patterns inside a function body so they\nparse correctly. This means:\n\n- **Expression patterns just work**: `print($A)`, `$X.add($B)`, `AppIcons.$FIELD`\n- **Statement patterns just work**: `if ($C) { $$$BODY }`, `return $A`, `var $X = $Y`\n- **Declaration patterns work directly**: `class $A {}`, `void $NAME($$$) { $$$BODY }`\n- **Import patterns need quotes**: use `import '$URI'` not `import $URI`\n  (Dart imports require string literals; bare `$URI` is misinterpreted as an identifier)\n\n## Commands\n\n### Search (read-only)\n\n```bash\n# Find all print() calls in the project\nast-grep run -p 'print($A)' -l dart\n\n# Find all setState calls with any arguments\nast-grep run -p 'setState($$$)' -l dart\n\n# Find member access on a specific class\nast-grep run -p 'AppIcons.$FIELD' -l dart\n\n# Find Navigator.push calls\nast-grep run -p 'Navigator.push($$$)' -l dart\n\n# Search specific directories only\nast-grep run -p 'print($A)' -l dart lib/\n\n# Output as JSON (for programmatic use)\nast-grep run -p 'print($A)' -l dart --json=stream\n\n# Show context around matches\nast-grep run -p 'print($A)' -l dart -C 3\n\n# Only show file paths with matches (like grep -l)\nast-grep run -p 'print($A)' -l dart --files-with-matches\n```\n\n### Rewrite (destructive — apply with care)\n\n```bash\n# Replace print() with debugPrint() everywhere, no confirmation\nast-grep run -p 'print($A)' -r 'debugPrint($A)' -l dart -U\n\n# Interactive mode — confirm each replacement\nast-grep run -p 'print($A)' -r 'debugPrint($A)' -l dart -i\n\n# Replace Navigator.push with GoRouter\nast-grep run -p 'Navigator.push(context, $ROUTE)' \\\n  -r 'context.push($ROUTE)' -l dart -U\n\n# Wrap widget construction in a builder\nast-grep run -p 'MyWidget($$$ARGS)' \\\n  -r 'Builder(builder: (_) => MyWidget($$$ARGS))' -l dart -U\n```\n\n### Debug a pattern\n\nWhen a pattern doesn't match what you expect, dump the parse tree:\n\n```bash\n# See how ast-grep parses your pattern\nast-grep run -p 'your_pattern_here' -l dart --debug-query=cst\n\n# See the AST (named nodes only)\nast-grep run -p 'your_pattern_here' -l dart --debug-query=ast\n```\n\n### Kind-based search\n\nMatch by AST node kind instead of pattern (useful when patterns are awkward):\n\n```bash\n# Find all if-statements\nast-grep run -k 'if_statement' -l dart\n\n# Find all class declarations\nast-grep run -k 'class_definition' -l dart\n\n# Combine kind selector with pattern for sub-matching\nast-grep run -p 'import $$$' --selector 'import_specification' -l dart\n```\n\n## Common Flutter patterns\n\n```bash\n# Find all StatefulWidget classes\nast-grep run -p 'class $NAME extends StatefulWidget { $$$BODY }' -l dart\n\n# Find all build methods\nast-grep run -p 'Widget build(BuildContext $CTX) { $$$BODY }' -l dart\n\n# Find all initState overrides\nast-grep run -p 'void initState() { $$$BODY }' -l dart\n\n# Find BlocProvider usage\nast-grep run -p 'BlocProvider.of<$TYPE>($CTX)' -l dart\n\n# Replace deprecated FlatButton with TextButton\nast-grep run -p 'FlatButton($$$ARGS)' -r 'TextButton($$$ARGS)' -l dart -U\n\n# Find dispose methods (check for missing super.dispose)\nast-grep run -p 'void dispose() { $$$BODY }' -l dart\n```\n\n## Tips\n\n1. **Start with search, then add rewrite** — always verify matches before `-r`\n2. **Use `--json=stream`** when piping to `jq` or processing programmatically\n3. **`$$$` is greedy** — it captures everything to the end of the argument list / block\n4. **Semicolons are optional** in patterns — ast-grep handles them automatically\n5. **Quotes matter for strings** — `'hello'` in a pattern matches the literal string `'hello'`\n6. **`-l dart` is required** — ast-grep won't auto-detect Dart without it (or use file extension inference with `--globs '*.dart'`)\n7. **Metavar names are UPPERCASE** — `$name` won't work, use `$NAME`\n"
}
~~~~

**There it is.** Line 200-202: `SgLang::build_pattern` uses the default `builder.build(...)` — it does NOT delegate to the underlying `Dart::build_pattern`. Our custom wrapping logic is completely bypassed by the CLI.

I need to fix this. `SgLang::build_pattern` should delegate to the inner language's `build_pattern` instead of reimplementing it.

## Assistant

*2026-06-02 18:12:11 UTC*

Line 433: `S::Dart => Dart.$method(...)` — this dispatches to our custom `Dart::build_pattern`. So `SupportLang::build_pattern` is correct. The problem is that `SgLang::build_pattern` on line 200-202 of `mod.rs` **does NOT delegate** to `SupportLang::build_pattern`. It reimplements with the default behavior.

The fix is simple:

---

## Assistant

*2026-06-02 18:12:13 UTC*

**Tool: Edit** — `toolu_012wUhgQHZwmtMUSDzwuc7c2`

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/me/Desktop/workspace/ast-grep/crates/cli/src/lang/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "  fn build_pattern(&self, builder: &PatternBuilder) -> std::result::Result<Pattern, PatternError> {\n    builder.build(|src| StrDoc::try_new(src, *self))\n  }",
  "new_string": "  fn build_pattern(&self, builder: &PatternBuilder) -> std::result::Result<Pattern, PatternError> {\n    match self {\n      Builtin(b) => b.build_pattern(builder),\n      Custom(c) => c.build_pattern(builder),\n    }\n  }"
}
```

## Assistant

*2026-06-02 18:14:20 UTC*

Everything works. Here's what happened:

1. **Found a critical bug**: `SgLang::build_pattern` in the CLI was bypassing all custom language `build_pattern` implementations — it used the default `builder.build()` instead of delegating to `Builtin(b).build_pattern()`. This meant our Dart wrapping logic never ran from the actual `ast-grep` / `sg` binary.

2. **Fixed it**: Changed the CLI's `SgLang::build_pattern` to delegate to the inner language (`Builtin` or `Custom`).

3. **Reinstalled**: The binary now correctly wraps Dart expression patterns.

4. **Verified**: `if ($COND) { $$$BODY }`, `setState($$$)`, `print($A)`, and `class ... extends StatefulWidget` all match real Flutter code.

The skill at `~/.claude/skills/ast-grep-dart/SKILL.md` is live and registered. It'll teach any fresh Claude session how to use `ast-grep` with Dart/Flutter.

~~~~
1	# ast-grep Development Instructions
2
3	ast-grep is a CLI tool for structural search, lint, and code rewriting using abstract syntax trees. It's written in Rust with TypeScript/Node.js (NAPI) and Python bindings.
4
5	**Always reference these instructions first and fallback to search or bash commands only when you encounter unexpected information that does not match the info here.**
6
7	## Working Effectively
8
9	### Essential Setup and Build Commands
10	Run these commands in the repository root (the top-level directory of your local clone, e.g. `<repo-root>`) in order:
11
12	- Check Rust toolchain: `rustc --version && cargo --version`
13	- Quick validation: `cargo check` - takes 2m 40s, validates dependencies and compilation
14	- Debug build: `cargo build` - takes 42 seconds. **NEVER CANCEL.** Set timeout to 120+ seconds.
15	- Release build: `cargo build --release` - takes 2m 26s. **NEVER CANCEL.** Set timeout to 180+ seconds.
16	- Test suite: `cargo test` - takes 48 seconds, runs 94+ tests. **NEVER CANCEL.** Set timeout to 120+ seconds.
17
18	### Linting and Formatting
19	- Format check: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` - takes <1 second
20	- Lint check: `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features --workspace --release --locked -- -D clippy::all` - takes 30 seconds. **NEVER CANCEL.** Set timeout to 60+ seconds.
21
22	### Running the CLI
23	- Main binary: `./target/release/ast-grep --help`
24	- Short alias: `./target/debug/sg --version` (requires ast-grep in PATH: `PATH="$(pwd)/target/debug:$PATH"`)
25	- Test pattern matching: `./target/release/ast-grep -p 'console.log($MSG)' -l js /path/to/file.js`
26	- Test rewriting: `./target/release/ast-grep -p 'var $VAR = $VALUE' -r 'let $VAR = $VALUE' -l js /path/to/file.js`
27
28	## Node.js/NAPI Bindings
29
30	### Setup and Build
31	- Navigate to: `cd crates/napi`
32	- Install dependencies: `yarn install` - takes 41 seconds. **NEVER CANCEL.** Set timeout to 90+ seconds.
33	- Debug build: `yarn build:debug` - takes 37 seconds. **NEVER CANCEL.** Set timeout to 90+ seconds.
34	- Test suite: `yarn test` - takes 5 seconds, runs 42 tests. **NEVER CANCEL.** Set timeout to 30+ seconds.
35	- Lint (requires network): `yarn lint` - may fail due to network restrictions, this is expected
36
37	**Note:** The NAPI bindings require Node.js >= 10. Tested with Node.js v20.19.4 and yarn 1.22.22.
38
39	## Python Bindings
40
41	### Setup and Build
42	- Install maturin: `pip install maturin`
43	- Build wheel: `maturin build -m crates/pyo3/Cargo.toml` - takes 21 seconds. **NEVER CANCEL.** Set timeout to 60+ seconds.
44	- Install wheel: `pip install target/wheels/ast_grep_py-*.whl`
45	- Test import: `python3 -c "import ast_grep_py; print('Python bindings work!')"`
46
47	**Note:** Python tests require module to be built in development mode, which needs virtualenv. Use wheel installation instead.
48
49	## Validation Scenarios
50
51	### After Making Changes - ALWAYS run these scenarios:
52	1. **Basic CLI functionality test:**
53	   ```bash
54	   echo 'console.log("hello world")' > /tmp/test.js
55	   ./target/release/ast-grep -p 'console.log($MSG)' -l js /tmp/test.js
56	   # Should output: /tmp/test.js with line match
57	   ```
58
59	2. **Rewrite functionality test:**
60	   ```bash
61	   echo 'var x = 5;' > /tmp/test.js
62	   ./target/release/ast-grep -p 'var $VAR = $VALUE' -r 'let $VAR = $VALUE' -l js /tmp/test.js
63	   # Should show diff replacing var with let
64	   ```
65
66	3. **Configuration-based scanning test:**
67	   ```bash
68	   mkdir -p /tmp/test-project/src && cd /tmp/test-project
69	   echo 'var y = 10;' > src/test.js
70	   cat > sgconfig.yml << 'EOF'
71	   rule:
72	     id: no-var
73	     message: Use let or const instead of var
74	     severity: error
75	     language: javascript
76	     rule:
77	       pattern: var $VAR = $VALUE
78	     fix: let $VAR = $VALUE
79	   EOF
80	   ./target/release/ast-grep scan src/
81	   ```
82
83	4. **LSP server startup test:**
84	   ```bash
85	   timeout 5 ./target/release/ast-grep lsp || echo "LSP started and stopped as expected"
86	   # Should show configuration error (expected) or start LSP server
87	   ```
88
89	### Pre-commit validation checklist:
90	- [ ] `cargo fmt --all -- --check` (format check)
91	- [ ] `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features --workspace --release --locked -- -D clippy::all` (lint check)
92	- [ ] `cargo test` (test suite)
93	- [ ] Run validation scenarios above
94	- [ ] If touching NAPI code: `cd crates/napi && yarn test`
95
96	## Common Issues and Solutions
97
98	### Build Issues
99	- **"No such file or directory" for sg binary:** Ensure ast-grep is in PATH or use full path to sg binary
100	- **Network errors during yarn lint:** Expected in restricted environments, dprint needs internet access
101	- **Python test failures:** Expected, tests require development build which needs virtualenv
102
103	### Performance Expectations
104	- **CRITICAL:** Builds can take 2+ minutes - **NEVER CANCEL** build or test commands
105	- Set timeouts: Debug build (120s+), Release build (180s+), Tests (120s+), Clippy (60s+)
106	- Large dependency downloads on first build are normal
107
108	## Repository Structure
109
110	### Key Directories
111	- `crates/cli/` - Main CLI application
112	- `crates/core/` - Core AST processing logic
113	- `crates/config/` - Configuration handling
114	- `crates/language/` - Language-specific parsers
115	- `crates/napi/` - Node.js bindings
116	- `crates/pyo3/` - Python bindings
117	- `crates/lsp/` - Language Server Protocol implementation
118	- `fixtures/` - Test fixtures
119	- `npm/` - NPM package distribution
120
121	### Important Files
122	- `Cargo.toml` - Workspace configuration
123	- `crates/cli/Cargo.toml` - Main CLI package config
124	- `crates/napi/package.json` - Node.js package config
125	- `pyproject.toml` - Python package config
126	- `.github/workflows/` - CI/CD pipelines
127
128	## Supported Languages
129	ast-grep supports 20+ programming languages including JavaScript, TypeScript, Python, Rust, Go, Java, C/C++, and more through tree-sitter parsers.
130
131	## Additional Notes
132	- The project uses tree-sitter for parsing
133	- Multiple output formats supported: colored terminal, JSON, etc.
134	- Interactive mode available for applying fixes
135	- LSP server for editor integration
136	- WASM version available for web usage
~~~~

```
+/// Dart's tree-sitter grammar only accepts declarations at the top level.
+/// Expression-level patterns (function calls, member access, assignments, etc.)
+/// fail to parse when given as bare source, since they are not valid top-level
+/// Dart constructs. This implementation wraps such patterns inside a function
+/// body and extracts the inner node so that expression/statement patterns work.
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
+pub struct Dart;

-fn test_non_match(query: &str, source: &str) {

+const WRAPPER_PREFIX: &str = "void _() {\n";
+const WRAPPER_SUFFIX: &str = "\n}";

+impl Language for Dart {
+  fn kind_to_id(&self, kind: &str) -> u16 {
+    self.get_ts_language().id_for_node_kind(kind, true)
+  }
+  fn field_to_id(&self, field: &str) -> Option<u16> {
+    self
+      .get_ts_language()
+      .field_id_for_name(field)
+      .map(|f| f.get())
+  }
+  fn build_pattern(&self, builder: &PatternBuilder) -> Result<Pattern, PatternError> {
+    let src = builder.src();
+    // Check if the pattern parses cleanly as a top-level construct.
+    // Dart's grammar can misinterpret expressions as declarations
+    // (e.g. `print($A)` becomes a function_signature, `AppIcons.$FIELD`
+    // becomes a type reference).  If the direct parse has any ERROR or
+    // MISSING nodes, try wrapping in a function body instead.
+    if self.should_try_wrapping(src) {
+      if let Some(pattern) = self.try_wrapped_pattern(src) {
+        return Ok(pattern);
+      }
+    }
+    builder.build(|s| StrDoc::try_new(s, *self))
+  }
 }
+/// Dart keywords that begin statements but are not valid function/type names.
+/// When tree-sitter misinterprets `if (...)` as a function declaration named
+/// "if", this list lets us detect and correct the misparse.
+const DART_STATEMENT_KEYWORDS: &[&str] = &[
+  "if", "else", "for", "while", "do", "switch", "try", "throw", "rethrow", "return", "break",
+  "continue", "yield", "await", "assert",
+];
+
+impl Dart {
+  /// Decides whether the direct (top-level) parse is likely wrong and
+  /// wrapping should be attempted.  Four independent signals:
+  ///  1. Root node itself is ERROR (total misparse)
+  ///  2. A direct child of source_file is ERROR
+  ///  3. Any MISSING node in the tree (grammar hallucinated a token like `;`)
+  ///  4. Source starts with a statement keyword misread as a function name
+  fn should_try_wrapping(&self, src: &str) -> bool {
+    let Ok(doc) = StrDoc::try_new(src, *self) else {
+      return true;
+    };
+    let root = Root::doc(doc);
+    let root_node = root.root();
+    if root_node.is_error() {
+      return true;
+    }
+    if root_node.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {
+      return true;
+    }
+    if Self::subtree_has_missing(&root_node) {
+      return true;
+    }
+    let first_word = src
+      .split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric() && c != '_')
+      .next()
+      .unwrap_or("");
+    DART_STATEMENT_KEYWORDS.contains(&first_word)
+  }
+
+  /// Returns true if any node in the subtree is a MISSING (phantom) node
+  /// inserted by tree-sitter to recover from a parse error.
+  fn subtree_has_missing<D: ast_grep_core::Doc>(node: &ast_grep_core::Node<'_, D>) -> bool {
+    if node.is_missing() {
+      return true;
+    }
+    node.children().any(|c| Self::subtree_has_missing(&c))
+  }
+
+  /// Wraps `src` inside a function body and tries to extract the inner
+  /// statement or expression as a pattern. Tries without a trailing semicolon
+  /// first (for compound statements like if/for/while), then with one (for
+  /// expression statements like function calls and assignments).
+  fn try_wrapped_pattern(&self, src: &str) -> Option<Pattern> {
+    // Try without semicolon first (statement patterns like if/for/while),
+    // then with semicolon (expression patterns that need `;` to form a
+    // complete statement in Dart).
+    let candidates = [
+      format!("{WRAPPER_PREFIX}{src}{WRAPPER_SUFFIX}"),
+      format!("{WRAPPER_PREFIX}{src};{WRAPPER_SUFFIX}"),
+    ];
+    for wrapped in &candidates {
+      if let Some(pattern) = self.try_extract_inner(wrapped) {
+        return Some(pattern);
+      }
+    }
+    None
+  }
+
+  /// Parses the wrapped source, locates the wrapper function's block, and
+  /// extracts the first statement/expression as a [`Pattern`]. Returns `None`
+  /// if the parse contains errors or no usable inner node is found.
+  fn try_extract_inner(&self, wrapped: &str) -> Option<Pattern> {
+    let doc = StrDoc::try_new(wrapped, *self).ok()?;
+    let root = Root::doc(doc);
+    let root_node = root.root();
+    let block_kind = KindMatcher::new("block", *self);
+    let block = root_node.find(&block_kind)?;
+    if block.children().any(|c| c.is_error()) {
+      return None;
+    }
+    let inner = block.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?;
+    // Unwrap expression_statement to get the inner expression so that
+    // `print($A)` matches `call_expression` nodes directly.
+    let target = if inner.kind() == "expression_statement" {
+      inner.children().find(|c| c.is_named())?
+    } else {
+      inner
+    };
+    let mut pattern = Pattern::from(target);
+    if pattern.has_error() {
+      return None;
+    }
+    // Dart's grammar sometimes parses `{ $$$BODY }` as set_or_map_literal
+    // instead of block (ambiguity when content looks like an expression).
+    // Fix up the pattern tree so it matches real block nodes.
+    let set_or_map_id = self.kind_to_id("set_or_map_literal");
+    let block_id = self.kind_to_id("block");
+    let expr_stmt_id = self.kind_to_id("expression_statement");
+    Self::fix_set_literal_to_block(&mut pattern.node, set_or_map_id, block_id, expr_stmt_id);
+    Some(pattern)
+  }

-#[test]
-fn test_dart_class_with_body() {
-  test_match("class $A { $$$BODY }", "class Foo { int x = 1; }");
+  /// Recursively replace set_or_map_literal nodes with block nodes in the
+  /// pattern tree. This fixes the Dart grammar ambiguity where `{ $$$BODY }`
+  /// is parsed as a set literal instead of a block in statement contexts.
+  fn fix_set_literal_to_block(
+    node: &mut PatternNode,
+    set_or_map_id: u16,
+    block_id: u16,
+    expr_stmt_id: u16,
+  ) {
+    if let PatternNode::Internal { kind_id, children } = node {
+      // An expression_statement wrapping a set_or_map_literal should
+      // become just a block (remove the expression_statement wrapper).
+      if *kind_id == expr_stmt_id {
+        if let Some(first_named) = children.iter().position(
+          |c| matches!(c, PatternNode::Internal { kind_id: k, .. } if *k == set_or_map_id),
+        ) {
+          if let PatternNode::Internal {
+            kind_id: inner_kind,
+            children: inner_children,
+          } = &mut children[first_named]
+          {
+            *inner_kind = block_id;
+            // Promote the block to replace the expression_statement
+            let block_children = std::mem::take(inner_children);
+            *kind_id = block_id;
+            *children = block_children;
+            return;
+          }
+        }
+      }
+      if *kind_id == set_or_map_id {
+        *kind_id = block_id;
+      }
+      for child in children.iter_mut() {
+        Self::fix_set_literal_to_block(child, set_or_map_id, block_id, expr_stmt_id);
+      }
+    }
+  }
 }

+#[cfg(test)]
+mod test {
+  use super::*;
+  use crate::test::{test_match_lang, test_non_match_lang, test_replace_lang};
+
+  fn test_match(query: &str, source: &str) {
+    test_match_lang(query, source, Dart);
+  }
+
+  fn test_non_match(query: &str, source: &str) {
+    test_non_match_lang(query, source, Dart);
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_class() {
+    test_match("class $A {}", "class Foo {}");
+    test_non_match("class $A {}", "class Foo { int x = 1; }");
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_class_with_body() {
+    test_match("class $A { $$$BODY }", "class Foo { int x = 1; }");
+  }
+
+  fn test_replace(src: &str, pattern: &str, replacer: &str) -> String {
+    test_replace_lang(src, pattern, replacer, Dart)
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_replace() {
+    let ret = test_replace("class Foo {}", "class $A {}", "class $A extends Base {}");
+    assert_eq!(ret, "class Foo extends Base {}");
+  }
+
+  // -- Expression-level patterns (new tests) --
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_function_call() {
+    test_match("print($A)", "void main() { print(123); }");
+    test_match("print($$$)", "void main() { print(1, 2, 3); }");
+    test_non_match("print($A)", "void main() { debugPrint(123); }");
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_method_call() {
+    test_match("$X.add($A)", "void f() { list.add(1); }");
+    test_non_match("$X.add($A)", "void f() { list.remove(1); }");
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_named_constructor() {
+    test_match(
+      "OverlayButton.bubble($$$)",
+      "class Foo { Widget build() { return OverlayButton.bubble(onTap: f); } }",
+    );
+    test_non_match(
+      "OverlayButton.bubble($$$)",
+      "class Foo { Widget build() { return OverlayButton.story(onTap: f); } }",
+    );
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_assignment() {
+    test_match("$A = $B", "void f() { x = 42; }");
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_variable_declaration() {
+    test_match("var $A = $B", "void f() { var x = 42; }");
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_import() {
+    // Dart imports use string literals, so $URI must be quoted.
+    // Bare `import $URI` is misinterpreted by tree-sitter as a
+    // variable declaration (type: import, name: $URI).
+    test_match("import '$URI'", "import 'package:flutter/material.dart';");
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_top_level_function() {
+    test_match(
+      "void $NAME($$$) { $$$BODY }",
+      "void main() { print('hello'); }",
+    );
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_member_access() {
+    test_match("AppIcons.$FIELD", "void f() { var x = AppIcons.heart; }");
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_return_statement() {
+    test_match("return $A", "int f() { return 42; }");
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_if_statement() {
+    test_match(
+      "if ($COND) { $$$BODY }",
+      "void f() { if (x > 0) { print(x); } }",
+    );
+  }
+
+  #[test]
+  fn test_dart_expression_replace() {
+    let ret = test_replace("void f() { print(123); }", "print($A)", "debugPrint($A)");
+    assert_eq!(ret, "void f() { debugPrint(123); }");
+  }
 }
```

```
## Summary
- Add custom `build_pattern` for Dart that wraps expression/statement patterns in a function body, fixing misparses caused by Dart's declaration-only top-level grammar
- Fix `SgLang::build_pattern` to delegate to underlying language impls instead of bypassing them
- Handle Dart's `{ $$$BODY }` set_or_map_literal vs block ambiguity via pattern tree post-processing

## Test plan
- 14 Dart-specific tests covering declarations, expressions, statements, rewrites
- Full suite (645+ tests) passes with 0 failures
- Verified against real Flutter project: `if`, `setState`, `StatefulWidget` patterns all match
- `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy -D clippy::all` clean
```

## Assistant

*2026-06-02 19:48:41 UTC*

Merging into your fork's main is better. The changes touch 5 files, and only one (`dart.rs`) sees regular upstream activity — the others are single-line additions. Rebasing a long-lived feature branch gets tedious fast since you'd have to remember to do it, and every merge conflict shows up as "feature branch is behind" noise.

With the changes on your fork's main:
- `git pull upstream main && git push` is your whole update workflow
- Conflicts will be rare and obvious when they happen (the `execute_lang_method!` macro or `SgLang::build_pattern`)
- `cargo install --path crates/cli` after pulling always gives you the latest upstream + your Dart fixes
